r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
An integer sequence of length between 1 and N, inclusive, where each element is between 1 and M, inclusive, is called a good sequence.
The score of a good sequence is defined as the number of positive divisors of X, where X is the product of the elements in the sequence.
There are \displaystyle \sum_{k=1}^{N}M^k good sequences. Find the sum of the scores of all those sequences modulo 998244353.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M

Output

Print the answer as an integer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq N \leq 10^{18}
- 1 \leq M \leq 16
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

1 7

Sample Output 1

16

There are seven good sequences: (1),(2),(3),(4),(5),(6),(7). Their scores are 1,2,2,3,2,4,2, respectively, so the answer is 1+2+2+3+2+4+2=16.

Sample Input 2

3 11

Sample Output 2

16095

For example, (8,11) and (1,8,2) are good sequences. Here is the process of calculating their scores:

- The product of the elements in (8,11) is 8 \times 11 = 88. 88 has eight positive divisors: 1,2,4,8,11,22,44,88, so the score of (8,11) is 8.
- The product of the elements in (1,8,2) is 1 \times 8 \times 2 = 16. 16 has five positive divisors: 1,2,4,8,16, so the score of (1,8,2) is 5.

Sample Input 3

81131 14

Sample Output 3

182955659

Remember to take the result modulo 998244353.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, can we compute U_r more directly using subset DP. Let's detail.

Let nprimes = 6, full = 1<<nprimes.
C = [0]*full
for mask in range(full):
    if mask ==0: C[mask]=M
    else:
       total=0
       for a in range(1,M+1):
           prod=1
           for i,p in enumerate(primes):
               if mask>>i &1:
                   # compute v_p(a)
                   cnt=0; x=a; while x%p==0: cnt++; x//=p
                   prod *= cnt
                   if prod==0: break
           total += prod
       C[mask]=total%MOD
But M<=16, can precompute exponent matrix exp[a][i] to avoid repeated factoring.

Simpler:
exp = [[0]*6 for _ in range(M+1)]
for a in range(1,M+1):
   x=a
   for i,p in enumerate(primes):
      cnt=0
      while x%p==0:
         cnt+=1; x//=p
      exp[a][i]=cnt
Then for mask:
  total=0
  for a in 1..M:
    prod=1
    for i in range(6):
      if mask>>i &1:
         prod *= exp[a][i]
    total += prod
  C[mask]=total%MOD
Since M small, okay. Note for a=1, exp all 0, product for nonempty mask 0; for empty mask product=1, total M.

Then dp:
dp = [[0]*full for _ in range(7)]
dp[0][0]=1
for r in range(1,7):
    for mask in range(1,full):
        # find least set bit
        lsb = mask & -mask
        # iterate submasks T of mask that contain lsb
        sub = mask
        while sub:
            if sub & lsb:
                dp[r][mask] = (dp[r][mask] + C[sub] * dp[r-1][mask ^ sub]) % MOD
            sub = (sub-1) & mask

Let's think about the mathematical expansion more rigorously to ensure no missing factor for positions not assigned.

Original:
F_k = ∑_{a_1..a_k} ∏_{p∈P} (1 + ∑_{i=1}^k v_p(a_i)).
Expand product over p:
= ∑_{A⊆P} ∑_{a_1..a_k} ∏_{p∈A} (∑_{i=1}^k v_p(a_i)).
For fixed A, expand ∏_{p∈A} ∑_{i=1}^k v_p(a_i) = ∑_{j: A→[k]} ∏_{p∈A} v_p(a_{j(p)}).
Then sum over sequences:
For each position i, if no p maps to i, factor ∑_{a_i}1 = M. If p maps to i, factor ∑_{a_i} ∏_{p∈j^{-1}(i)} v_p(a_i) = C(fiber). Thus contribution = M^{k-r} ∏_{blocks} C(block), where r = number of nonempty fibers. The fibers form a set partition π of A. For a given set partition π with r blocks, how many functions j have exactly these fibers? Each block B∈π is assigned a distinct position. Blocks are distinguishable by content, so number = k(k-1)...(k-r+1) = (k)_r. Therefore:
F_k = ∑_{r=0}^{|P|} (k)_r M^{k-r} ∑_{A⊆P} ∑_{π∈Π_r(A)} ∏_{B∈π} C(B).
Yes. Good.

def apply_power(mat, exp, vec):
    while exp:
        if exp&1:
            vec = mat_vec_mul(mat, vec)
        mat = mat_mul(mat, mat)
        exp >>=1
    return vec

Potential micro-optimization: Matrix multiplication with D=14 and modulo. We can implement as:
def mat_mul(A, B):
    n=len(A)
    res=[[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        for k in range(n):
            if A[i][k]:
                aik=A[i][k]
                Bk=B[k]
                for j in range(n):
                    res[i][j] = (res[i][j] + aik*Bk[j]) % MOD
    return res
Since D small, okay. But modulo inside inner loop might be okay. Could optimize by accumulating and mod after row:
for i:
  for k:
    if aik:
      for j:
        res[i][j] += aik*Bk[j]
  for j: res[i][j] %= MOD
But values can grow: aik,Bk < MOD, product ~1e18, sum over 14 terms ~1e19, Python big ints but still okay. 14 terms only, so no overflow in Python. We can reduce mod after each row to avoid huge. Let's implement:
def mat_mul(A,B):
    n=len(A)
    res=[[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        ri=res[i]
        for k,aik in enumerate(A[i]):
            if aik:
                Bk=B[k]
                for j in range(n):
                    ri[j] += aik * Bk[j]
        for j in range(n):
            ri[j] %= MOD
    return res
This does 14*14*14 multiplications, each product ~1e18, sum 14 terms ~1.4e19, Python int 64-bit? Actually Python arbitrary, but 1e19 fits in 64 bits? 2^63=9.22e18, 1.4e19 > 64-bit but Python handles; still small. But after each row mod. Good.

mat_vec_mul:
def mat_vec_mul(A,v):
    n=len(A)
    res=[0]*n
    for i,row in enumerate(A):
        s=0
        for j,a in enumerate(row):
            if a and v[j]:
                s += a*v[j]
        res[i]=s%MOD
    return res
Products ~1e18, sum 14 terms ~1.4e19, okay.

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data=sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N=int(data[0]); M=int(data[1])
    primes=[2,3,5,7,11,13]
    P=len(primes)
    full=1<<P

    # exponent matrix
    exp = [[0]*P for _ in range(M+1)]
    for a in range(1,M+1):
        x=a
        for i,p in enumerate(primes):
            cnt=0
            while x % p == 0:
                cnt += 1
                x //= p
            exp[a][i]=cnt
    # Note: if we modify x while looping primes, for a=12, x after dividing by 2 becomes3, then for p=3, x%3==0 yes. But for p=5, x remains3. This is okay because primes are increasing and x after removing smaller primes; but if x has factor of larger prime, it remains. For p=2, x=12 -> divide to3; p=3 -> divide to1. Good. But for a=16, x after p=2 becomes1; p=3 while x%3? 1%3 !=0. Good. However, for a=18 not in range. Since primes list includes all prime factors up to16, this works. But if x becomes 1, for later primes x%p !=0. Good.
    # However, for a=8, x after p=2 becomes1, p=3 no. Good.
    # But for a=10, p=2 x=5, p=3 x=5 no, p=5 x=5 -> divide to1. Good.
    # Need not reset x for each p? We use same x removing all smaller prime factors. Since primes are in increasing order, this correctly counts exponents. For p=2, count all factors 2. For p=3, x no longer has 2, but v3 unaffected. Good.

    C=[0]*full
    for mask in range(full):
        if mask==0:
            C[mask]=M % MOD
        else:
            total=0
            for a in range(1,M+1):
                prod=1
                for i in range(P):
                    if mask>>i &1:
                        prod *= exp[a][i]
                        if prod==0:
                            break
                total += prod
            C[mask]=total % MOD

    dp = [[0]*full for _ in range(P+1)] # P+1=7
    dp[0][0]=1
    for r in range(1,P+1):
        for mask in range(1,full):
            lsb = mask & -mask
            total=0
            sub=mask
            while sub:
                if sub & lsb:
                    total = (total + C[sub] * dp[r-1][mask ^ sub]) % MOD
                sub = (sub-1) & mask
            dp[r][mask]=total
    U=[0]*(P+1)
    for r in range(P+1):
        U[r]=sum(dp[r]) % MOD

    D=14
    Tmat = [[0]*D for _ in range(D)]
    Mmod = M % MOD
    for r in range(7):
        # a_r row
        if r==0:
            Tmat[0][0] = Mmod
        else:
            Tmat[r][r] = Mmod
            Tmat[r][r-1] = (Mmod * r) % MOD
        # P_r row
        row = 7+r
        Tmat[row][row] = 1
        Tmat[row][r] = (Tmat[row][r] + Mmod) % MOD
        if r>0:
            Tmat[row][r-1] = (Tmat[row][r-1] + Mmod * r) % MOD

    vec=[0]*D
    vec[0]=Mmod
    vec[1]=Mmod
    vec[7]=Mmod
    vec[8]=Mmod

    expN = N-1
    # matrix exponent apply
    while expN:
        if expN & 1:
            vec = mat_vec_mul(Tmat, vec)
        expN >>= 1
        if expN:
            Tmat = mat_mul(Tmat, Tmat)
    # Wait: This squares only after shifting if expN nonzero. But is it correct if expN bit was set and then shifted? Yes as above. But note if expN initially e.g. 2: first iteration expN=2, bit0=0, expN>>=1 =>1, square Tmat=T^2. Next iteration expN=1, bit set, vec=T^2*vec, expN=0, no square. Good. If expN=1: bit set vec=T*vec, expN=0, no square. Good. If expN=0, no loop. Good.
    # However, standard binary exponent with base squaring after shift works only if we square after shifting, but we must square base for next bit regardless of whether current bit was set. We do if expN after shift nonzero. Good.

    invM = pow(Mmod, MOD-2, MOD)
    invpow=[1]*7
    for r in range(1,7):
        invpow[r] = invpow[r-1] * invM % MOD

    ans=0
    for r in range(7):
        Pr = vec[7+r]
        ans = (ans + U[r] * Pr % MOD * invpow[r]) % MOD
    print(ans)

Let's write final code:

import sys
MOD = 998244353

def mat_mul(A, B):
    n = len(A)
    res = [[0]*n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        ri = res[i]
        for k in range(n):
            aik = A[i][k]
            if aik:
                Bk = B[k]
                for j in range(n):
                    ri[j] += aik * Bk[j]
        for j in range(n):
            ri[j] %= MOD
    return res

def mat_vec_mul(A, v):
    n = len(A)
    res = [0]*n
    for i in range(n):
        s = 0
        row = A[i]
        for j in range(n):
            if row[j] and v[j]:
                s += row[j] * v[j]
        res[i] = s % MOD
    return res

def main():
   ...

if __name__ == "__main__": main()

But wait A[i][k] and Bk[j] are modulo <1e9, product <1e18, 14 terms <1.4e19, yes. However, after row mod, values <MOD. Good. If we square repeatedly, values remain mod. Good.

Let's implement DP:
dp = [[0]*full for _ in range(P+1)]
dp[0][0] = 1
for r in range(1, P+1):
    for mask in range(1, full):
        lsb = mask & -mask
        total = 0
        sub = mask
        while sub:
            if sub & lsb:
                total += C[sub] * dp[r-1][mask ^ sub]
            sub = (sub - 1) & mask
        dp[r][mask] = total % MOD
U = [sum(dp[r]) % MOD for r in range(P+1)]

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "N" maybe 1 and exp=1; loop applies one transition. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "M=1" and invM=1; T_r = P_r. But U_r=0 for r>0, answer=P0=N. Matrix with M=1:
T
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353


def mat_mul(A, B):
    n = len(A)
    res = [[0] * n for _ in range(n)]
    for i in range(n):
        ri = res[i]
        for k in range(n):
            aik = A[i][k]
            if aik:
                Bk = B[k]
                for j in range(n):
                    ri[j] += aik * Bk[j]
        for j in range(n):
            ri[j] %= MOD
    return res


def mat_vec_mul(A, v):
    n = len(A)
    res = [0] * n
    for i in range(n):
        s = 0
        row = A[i]
        for j in range(n):
            if row[j] and v[j]:
                s += row[j] * v[j]
        res[i] = s % MOD
    return res


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    N = int(data[0])
    M = int(data[1])

    primes = [2, 3, 5, 7, 11, 13]
    P = len(primes)
    full = 1 << P

    # vp[a][i] = exponent of primes[i] in a
    vp = [[0] * P for _ in range(M + 1)]
    for a in range(1, M + 1):
        x = a
        for i, p in enumerate(primes):
            cnt = 0
            while x % p == 0:
                cnt += 1
                x //= p
            vp[a][i] = cnt

    # C[mask] = sum_{a=1..M} product_{p in mask} v_p(a)
    C = [0] * full
    for mask in range(full):
        if mask == 0:
            C[mask] = M % MOD
            continue
        total = 0
        for a in range(1, M + 1):
            prod = 1
            for i in range(P):
                if (mask >> i) & 1:
                    prod *= vp[a][i]
                    if prod == 0:
                        break
            total += prod
        C[mask] = total % MOD

    # dp[r][mask]: sum over set partitions of mask into r blocks
    dp = [[0] * full for _ in range(P + 1)]
    dp[0][0] = 1

    for r in range(1, P + 1):
        for mask in range(1, full):
            lsb = mask & -mask
            total = 0
            sub = mask
            while sub:
                if sub & lsb:
                    total += C[sub] * dp[r - 1][mask ^ sub]
                sub = (sub - 1) & mask
            dp[r][mask] = total % MOD

    U = [sum(dp[r]) % MOD for r in range(P + 1)]

    # Matrix for computing P_r(N) = sum_{k=1..N} (k)_r M^k
    # State: a_0..a_6, P_0..P_6, where a_r(k) = (k)_r M^k
    D = 14
    T = [[0] * D for _ in range(D)]
    Mmod = M % MOD

    for r in range(7):
        # a_r(k+1)
        if r == 0:
            T[0][0] = Mmod
        else:
            T[r][r] = Mmod
            T[r][r - 1] = (Mmod * r) % MOD

        # P_r(k+1)
        row = 7 + r
        T[row][row] = 1
        T[row][r] = (T[row][r] + Mmod) % MOD
        if r > 0:
            T[row][r - 1] = (T[row][r - 1] + Mmod * r) % MOD

    # Start from k = 0: a_0(0) = 1, all P_r(0) = 0
    vec = [0] * D
    vec[0] = 1

    exp = N
    while exp:
        if exp & 1:
            vec = mat_vec_mul(T, vec)
        T = mat_mul(T, T)
        exp >>= 1

    invM = pow(Mmod, MOD - 2, MOD)
    invpow = [1] * 7
    for r in range(1, 7):
        invpow[r] = invpow[r - 1] * invM % MOD

    ans = 0
    for r in range(7):
        Pr = vec[7 + r]  # sum_{k=1..N} (k)_r M^k
        Tr = Pr * invpow[r] % MOD  # sum_{k=1..N} (k)_r M^{k-r}
        ans = (ans + U[r] * Tr) % MOD

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
